- **ov004 ROM-check twins, more blockers.** A local `u8 *base = (u8 *)0x04000000;` with every card register written as `*(vu32 *)(base + off)` makes MWCC build addresses as `mov rX, #0x4000000; add` instead of loading them from the pool. But LICM then hoists `base + 0x4000` out of the read loop into a stack slot. The target instead derives `0x04000000` again in every iteration as `lsl rB, rOne, #26`, reusing the `1` it loaded to build `0xf7ff` (`rsb rX, rOne, #0xf800`). `#pragma optimization_level 1..3` and other compiler versions (`--all-compilers`) did not reproduce it either.

## Trailing loops, inline parameter widths and unmatched zero registers (main, round mainbig3)

- **A block-scoped pointer and index for a function's last loop.** `func_0204ffdc` (text box update, draft near-miss) reused the function-level `line` pointer and `i` for its final per-line drawing loop. Every callee-saved register in the function was rotated. Opening a block with its own `TextBoxLine *ln = box->lines; int j;` for that loop took it from 174 to 81 aligned diffs. Swapping two `BOOL` declarations then brought it to 58. This is the same idea as the per-case loop indices in round big3, but here it fixed the registers of code *before* the loop too.
- **`u32`, not `u8`, for a nibble passed to an inline.** In the IMA-ADPCM decoder of `func_0203244c` (NNS stream data loader, draft), `Decode(st, u8 data)` added a masked copy of each nibble in a new register. `u32 data` used the masked register directly (269 to 186). Reading `pred` before `index` in the inline fixed the load order.
- **Assign the advanced pointer before the copy to keep two registers.** The target has `mov r1, r2; addeq r1, r2, #4` and then copies a 4-byte header from `r2`. `src = work; if (off == 0) { *st = *(T *)work; src = work + 4; }` coalesced `src` with `work`. Writing `src = work + 4;` *before* the struct copy makes the two interfere, which gives the copy.
- **Spill-slot order is not always declaration order.** Unlike `func_ov006_021d6ff0` (round ovlo9), reordering the function-level declarations of `func_0203244c` (a `.c` file with 15 spilled locals) did not move a single slot. One local landed below the compiler's loop temporaries in our build, but above them in the target.
- **`readOffset = blockOffset; outSize = samples;` before a switch.** That order kept `samples` in a register across the switch dispatch, as in the target (169 to 162). Each case then used the value already in that register.
- **Data the disassembler did not relocate.** `func_0203244c` loads the ADPCM index and step tables from `0x0207574c` and `0x0207575c` (inside `data_02075728`), but `relocs.txt` has no entries for those pool words. A matching file will need `data_0207574c`/`data_0207575c` symbols and `kind:load` relocs at `0x02032c44`/`0x02032c48`.
- **A zero register that MWCC added instead of folding.** The text box target computes `y = r9 + ...` with `r9` set only by `mov r9, #0` at entry. The same register supplies the zero high word of every `(s64)` fixed-point multiply. No source form we tried left the add in. We tried a variable initialised once, reassigned in a loop, `+= 0`, a struct or array field, an inline parameter and a block-scoped copy. A second `dy = 0` in an unrelated branch did keep the add, but it moved the other registers.
- **`__pformatter` switch tree, continued.** We rebuilt the 18 labels in a small test switch. Adding any single extra label from `0x26` to `0xff` as a `default:` alias did not give the target's split, where the right subtree has a `0x64`–`0x75` table and a lone `0x78`. Neither did any pair in `0x60`–`0x7f`, or removing any one label.
- **Permuter on C++ with virtual structs.** `tools/permuter/classconv.py` only flattens `class` bodies. For headers that declare `struct TextWin { virtual ... }`, rename those structs to `class` after preprocessing. Also rename each virtual method to `vfunc_XX` by slot, at the declaration and at every call, before `classconv.py`/`conv.py`. Drop classes with constructors that the function does not use. The mainbig3 scratch folder has a script that does this (`pset_cpp.sh` and `vren.py`).
## Stack parameters loaded late (ov000, round lo12)

- **A `volatile` stack parameter is loaded where it is first used.** MWCC loads the stack parameters it keeps in registers in the prologue, before an early `if (skip) return;`. In `func_ov000_0215f970` the target loads the 11th parameter (`ldr fp, [sp, #0xe0]`) only after that early return, while the other stack parameters are loaded up front. Declaring that one parameter `const Ctx *volatile ctx0` and copying it once into a local after the check (`ctx = ctx0;`) moved the load, and a declaration-order search over the locals then closed the remaining register rotation (7 diffs to 0). Wrapping the body in a `static inline` or using the parameter directly did not move the load. The sibling `func_ov000_02173d9c` (no early return) had the same parameter in the wrong register; `volatile` on it took the draft from 11 to 4 diffs, and a declaration-order search finished it. Try this on any draft where only a late-loaded stack parameter and one temporary are swapped.
- **A pointer local for one inlined `PlayPart` call swaps a local with the inline's registers.** In `func_ov000_0216d14c` and `func_ov000_0216df28` a function-level coordinate (`x`, or `x`/`y`) and the model pointer or `res` local of the inlined `Model_PlayPart`/`LoModelPlayPart` were swapped between two callee-saved registers, and no type or declaration-order change helped. The permuter found the fix: declare a model pointer at the top of the function (`Model *m;`, first in the list) and pass it to exactly one of the calls (`m = LFLD(Model *, self, 0xc); Model_PlayPart(m, 13);`), leaving the other calls on the field expression. Which call takes it matters, so try each one with a script. In `0216df28` this left `x` and `y` swapped, and declaring `s32 y; s32 x;` (y first) with plain assignments finished it.
- **A load placed after a loop's entry check: rotate the loop by hand.** In `func_ov000_0216607c` the target loads `slot->list` after the `cmp n, #0; bls` that skips the loop and before the body (`mov lr, #0; bls; ldr r0, [r0, #4]`). Reading `slot->list[i]` in the body let LICM hoist it there, but into the wrong register; loading it into a local before the `for` put the load above the check. Only the hand-rotated form matched: `i = 0; if (i < n) { lst = slot->list; do { ... } while (++i < n); }`. The permuter's hints on the way were reusing the first loop's `u8 i` for the second loop (instead of a separate `j`) and a pointer local for `slot->list`.
## Load order of CSE'd fields and random statement-order search (overlays 2–8, round ovlo11)

- **Load CSE'd fields into locals, in the target's order.** In `func_ov003_021d1e8c` the distance check reads `self->pos` and `tgt->pos` once each. The values are used again later for `ex`/`ez`, so they stay in registers, and which value gets the callee-saved `sl` decided 6 aligned diffs. Declaration order, the order of `dx`/`dz` and the order of the `sq` terms did not fix it. Loading all six coordinates into block-scoped locals first did fix it, with the loads in one exact order (`tx, tz, sz, ty, sy, sx`), which is neither the source nor the target load order. Only the order of the load statements mattered: initialisers and separate declarations gave the same code. A random search over the 720 orders found it in 20 tries.
- **Search statement order and operand order together.** `func_ov002_021d1cf8` (7 diffs) swapped three scratch registers around three `FX_MUL`s. It matched only with both a new load order (`bx, k, y, bz, by`) and `FX_MUL(k, by)` instead of `FX_MUL(by, k)`. Either change alone made it worse (16 and 8). A random shuffle of the load statements, plus a random operand order for each multiply, found it on the first try. When the remaining diffs are scratch registers around a block of loads and arithmetic, this search (a few hundred compiles) is cheap.
- **Small vector initialisers on the stack follow statement order, but not directly.** In `func_ov003_021d3d28` the target stores `ofs.x`, `ofs.z`, `ofs.y` after loading `self->unk_6c`. Only `r += 0xccd; ofs.y = self->unk_6c; ofs.x = 0; ofs.z = 0; d = ...;` matched among all 120 orders of those five statements.
- **Arguments computed before a store block.** In the same function, the target loads the `(u8)` argument `self->unk_4c->unk_0a` *before* an `if (slot) swap(slot->a, slot->b)` that sits before the call. The swap's stores block that load from moving down, so the argument has to be read into a local before the swap in the source.
- **Hoisting case locals and climbing their declaration order** (big4) took `func_ov003_021d3d28` from 90 to 28 diff lines on its first pass (`BOOL hit; u16 c; s32 h; Actor *tgt; Slot *slot;` at function level). In that function, an `s32 h` passed to an `s16` parameter (`lsl/asr` at the call) and `restore = FALSE;` written after `t = self->unk_4c;` (not as an initialiser) fixed the rest.
- **Not solved this round.** In `func_ov003_021d112c` (13 aligned) the second loop's counter and `anm` are swapped between `r8` and `sl`. Declaration order (hill climb over 11 locals), where and whether `anm = NULL` is written, block-scoping `anm`, `if (x && x) {}` probes after every statement, other counter types and 1800 permuter iterations had no effect. The struct-copy tail (`ldrh r2; ldrh r1; strh; strh` for fields 8 and 10) did not come from a `{u16,u16}` sub-struct copy, temporaries of any type in either order, or `s16`/`u16` field types. With temporaries, the loads always came out with field 10 first.

## Function-scope case locals, store order and stopped constant propagation (overlays 9–15 and ov000 high, round big5)

- **Draft a big state machine with a structural diff first.** For `func_ov000_021ad8ac` (5.8 KB, draft `scratchpad/big5/d8ac/best6.cpp`) the first draft was 342 aligned diffs, but with every register name masked (`sdo.py`: replace `r0`–`r12`/`sb`/`sl`/`fp`/`ip` with `R` before the `SequenceMatcher`) only 92 were left. Fix those first; most of the rest was one rotation of `r6`/`r7`/`r8` through the whole function.
- **Moving two case-local variables to function scope fixed that rotation.** The hoisted constants (`1`, `0x8000`) and the one real function-level local (`u8 *cam = data_ov000_021ccd54 + 0xf4`) were rotated by one register everywhere. Moving `u16 ang; BOOL turn;` (used only in case 1) from the case block to function scope, declared before `cam`, took the draft from 193 to 71 aligned diffs. Of the six orders of the three declarations, only `ang, turn, cam` gave 71 (the others gave 90–193). Try this before anything local.
- **The target's store order is not the source order.** Two cases needed a source order that differs from the target's store order. `self->92 = 8; self->a0 = 0x519; self->9c >>= 1; self->9a0 = 0xee00;` gives the target's order with the `9a0` halfword store *first*. In another arm, `self->9c -= self->9c >> 5;` written *before* `self->a0 += FX_MUL(0x266 - self->a0, 0x414);` gave the target, which loads `9c` into `fp` early and stores `a0` first (12 to 6). Use `permblk.py` on any group of independent field updates, even when the target's order looks obvious.
- **No local for a read-modify-write.** `a0 = self->a0; if (turn) self->a0 = a0 + FX_MUL(0x52 - a0, 0x85); else ...` shifted the registers of both multiplies. Writing `self->a0 += FX_MUL(0x52 - self->a0, 0x85);` in each arm (the compiler still loads `a0` once, above the branch) took 26 to 14.
- **`switch` on a shifted `u16`.** `switch ((u16)(x + 0x1000) >> 13)` compiles to `lsl #16; lsr #16; asr #13`. The target's `lsl #16; lsr #29` came from `(u32)(u16)(x + 0x1000) >> 13`. The same holds for `-((u32)(u16)-d >> 1)` (`lsr #17` and no `asr`).
- **Multiply by 40 as shifts.** `x * 40` gave `mov #0x28; mul`. The target's `lsl r0, r1, #3; add r0, r0, r1, lsl #5` came from `(x << 5) + (x << 3)`. Write the shifts in the opposite order to the target's, since MWCC swaps the operands.
- **A value left unassigned in one arm is initialised at the top of the block.** The target set `r4 = 0` at the start of the block and loaded `-0x414`/`0x414` into it in two arms of an `if`/`else if`/`else`. The third arm never wrote it. `tgt = 0;` as the first statement of the block, and nothing in the third arm, matched.
- **Expanded copies instead of an inline give the stack-slot order.** The same "spawn dust" code with a `VecFx32` occurs twice in `func_ov000_021ad8ac`, and a third vector is used later. As a `static inline`, the two copies took the lower slots. Written out as block-scoped code at each site, the slots followed declaration order (first at `sp+0x34`, second at `sp+0x28`, the later vector at `sp+0x1c`), as in the target.
- **The doubled `cmp` at the switch head came out by itself.** The target has `cmp r2, #0xa; bgt; cmp r2, #0xa; ldr sl, =data; bge`: the address of a global used after a call in case 10 is hoisted between the two compares. The draft produced the same code with no special source, so a doubled `cmp` can also come from a hoisted constant, not only from a variable initialised before the `switch`.
- **Stopping constant propagation with a per-arm assignment.** In `func_ov000_021b9b14` (draft `scratchpad/big5/9b14/c.cpp`) the target keeps `0x68` in `r8` and computes `sub r2, r8, #9`. With `q = 0x68;` once after the `if`, MWCC folded it to `mov r2, #0x5f`. Assigning `q = 0x68;` at the end of *each* arm stops the propagation. The arms' final `mov r8, #0x68` are then tail-merged into one instruction at the join, as in the target. That draft is still 122 aligned diffs. Its 64-bit multiplies use a hoisted zero register (`mla rX, rY, r4`, `adc rX, rY, r4`) for the constant's high word in every path but one, and no source form reproduced that yet.
- **Load order again.** In the same function, writing `cur = cam->42;` before `f = self->b68;` (the target loads `f` first) moved the next halfword load from `r0` to `r1`, as in the target (14 to 12 aligned diffs). This is the scratch-register effect described in round big4. Of the last 6 diffs, the only thing left is the position of `mov r0, #imm` and `ldr r4, =tgt` around `str sl, [sp]` in two tail-merged arms. Neither statement permutations, a call wrapper inline nor 35 minutes of the permuter changed it.

## Spilled locals, loop temporaries and enum ternaries (main, round mainbig4)

These took the NNS stream loader `func_0203244c` (in `sndarc_stream_3.c`) from 162 aligned diffs to a match. The order below is the order they were found, and each step built on the one before it.

- **`unsigned long` for one spilled local moves the whole stack frame.** Reordering declarations in this `.c` file never moved a spill slot. Changing `u32 offset;` to `unsigned long offset;` (or `long`) did. `offset` then took the slot above the compiler's loop temporaries, as in the target, and every other slot moved into place (162 to 102). Only that variable's type mattered. Scan each spilled local with `long`/`unsigned long` and keep the one that helps. This is the "long loop counter" trick from round ovlo10, but for a spilled local that is not a loop counter.
- **Block-scoped pointer and counter for an inner decode loop.** The ADPCM decode reused the function's `src` and `i`. A new `u8 *in;` and `u32 k;` declared inside the `if (format == 2)` block swapped the `st`/`src` registers back (102 to 76). A block-scoped `st` alone made it worse. Brute-force which of the locals to scope and in what order.
- **Declaration order of an inline's locals.** In `StrmDecodeAdpcm`, declaring `int step; int pred; int index; int diff;` (step first) and then assigning them fixed the register rotation inside all four inlined copies (76 to 36). The declaration order inside a `static inline` matters as much as in the caller, and the function-level hill climber does not search it.
- **Name the loop bound to swap two LICM spill slots.** The target spills `blockOffset + samples` above `blockOffset & 1`. A block-scoped `u32 end = blockOffset + samples;`, used in both `k < (end & ~1)` and `k < end`, swapped the two slots (36 to 26).
- **`*dst++` twice and `in++` in the `for` increment** fixed the scheduling at the end of the loop body (26 to 19). `dst[0] = ...; dst[1] = ...; dst += 2;` and `in++` in the body did not.
- **Split a sum so a late field load stays late.** `filePos = a * b * c + readOffset + player->header.dataOffset;` hoisted the `dataOffset` load into a free callee-saved register. `filePos = a * b * c + readOffset; filePos += player->header.dataOffset;` made the target reload `player` from its spill slot after the `mla` (19 to 3).
- **`moveq #0; movne #1` comes from an enum ternary.** `fmt == 0 ? 0 : 1` gave `movne r0, #1; moveq r0, #0`. With the enum constants (`fmt == 0 ? NNS_SND_STRM_FORMAT_PCM8 : NNS_SND_STRM_FORMAT_PCM16`) the order matched the target. The callback parameter type did not matter, only the ternary's operands.
- **Compare order: store the call result first.** `if (player->readFn(...) != readSize)` gave `cmp r1, r0` (readSize first). `readLen = player->readFn(...); if (readLen != readSize)` gave the target's `cmp r0, r1`. The return type, the operand order and the casts did not change it.
- **Pool words the disassembler left as numbers.** The target's ADPCM tables (`0x0207574c`, `0x0207575c`) sit inside `data_02075728` and had no relocs. The match needed `data_0207574c`/`data_0207575c` in `symbols.txt` and `kind:load` relocs at `0x02032c44`/`0x02032c48`. This splits `data_02075728` to its 0x24 bytes (`nsbca_3.c`'s `pivotUtil_`).

Still open in this round:
- **`func_0204ffdc` (text box update, 52).** `s32 y = dy; y += ...;` keeps the add, but as `add r2, r0, #0`, so MWCC still propagates `dy == 0`. The target's `add r2, sb, r0` needs `dy` to stay a variable. A second `dy = 0` in the `unk_002 < 0` branch keeps it a variable but moves the other registers (85; a declaration-order climb only reached 81).
- **Register-only near-misses unaffected by every declaration-order or type change tried:** `func_02056e74` (4) and `func_0205cbf0` (6) both differ only in the registers of an inlined `(s64)a * b + 0x800 >> 12`: the target's `adds` writes a new register instead of updating the low word in place. `func_02039844` (3) puts the `0x20` constant in `r4` where we use `ip`.
- **Scheduling-only near-misses.** `func_0206e3a0` (2, one `strh` against the `orr` feeding the fog register) and `func_0205c234` (2, where the pool load of `data_02149efc` goes relative to two `strh`) did not move with inline wrappers, chained assignments, temporaries or `volatile`. Permuter runs of about 35 and 10 minutes found nothing.

## `delete` with inline destructors, and what the new tricks did not fix (ov000, round lo13)

- **`if (p != NULL) { if (p != NULL) { ...teardown...; free(p); } p = NULL; }` is a `delete` expression.** The doubled null test in `func_ov000_0218e12c` (heap push, tear down the actor manager, heap pop) comes from `delete mgr` on a class with an inline destructor and a class-specific `operator delete` that calls the free function: `struct ActorMgrD : ActorMgr { ~ActorMgrD() { while (head) ActorMgr_RemoveGroup(this, head); } static void operator delete(void *p) { func_0203de1c(p); } };` then `delete (ActorMgrD *)data_ov000_021ccd68;`. Written as static inlines, the draft was 58 diffs at the default level and 19 under `#pragma optimization_level 3`. With the `delete`, it is 18 at the *default* level, and the pragma makes it worse (42). The callee-saved registers of the group-level locals now match. The `saved` heap pointer and the manager pointer still sit below the innermost actor locals instead of above them. If a near-miss only gets close under a lower optimization level, look for a C++ construct like this before reaching for the pragma.
- **`if (a != NULL) a->vfunc_NN();` can be `delete a`.** For a class whose virtual destructor is its *k*-th virtual function, MWCC compiles `delete a` as `cmp r0, #0; ldr r1, [r0]; ldr r1, [r1, #4*k]; blx r1`. It passes no delete flag and calls no `operator delete`. Actor removal's `vfunc_24` call is therefore a `delete` through a destructor in slot 9. Rewriting it this way produced identical code, so either spelling works.
- **MWCC stores outgoing stack arguments before it loads the register arguments.** For `f(l->to, l->from, t, 1, 0x8000)` after another call, MWCC emits `mov r1, #0x8000; str r1, [sp]` first. The target `func_ov000_0218a5ec` emits `mov ip, #0x8000` with the `str` last, which means the register arguments were live first. No local, inline wrapper, prototype change, cast or temporary changed this (4 diffs). The matched Camera code gets `ip` only because all of r0–r2 already hold arguments from the preceding condition.
- **Two tricks with no effect here.** Round lo12 found two tricks: a pointer local passed to exactly one inlined call, and a `volatile` stack parameter. Scripted over every call site, the pointer local changed nothing in `02181c4c`, `0215e6f0`, `02189b44`, `0217e560`, `0214bcc4`, `021708ec`, `02161994` and `02152f8c`. None of these has an incoming stack parameter, so the `volatile` trick does not apply.
- **`0217a1f8`** matches at 0 only under `1.2/sp3` and `1.2/sp4`. Under every dsi compiler, `func(self, 0x78, -1)` becomes `mov r1, #0x78; sub r2, r1, #0x79`, and no constant spelling or `opt_*` pragma prevents that. `tools/mwcc_versions.py` supports per-file compilers, but so far it is used only for the prebuilt NitroSystem libraries. Using it for one game function would be guesswork, so the function stays unmatched.
## Scheduling after allocation, switch pivots and pointer-local searches (overlays 2–8, round ovlo12)

- **MWCC schedules after register allocation, so a reused register blocks a load from moving up.** In `func_ov003_021e83d0` (draft, 6 aligned) the target loads `offset.x` and `offset.y` into two registers before storing either. The draft gives both values `r2`, and the anti-dependence keeps `ld y` after `st x`. The constructor form, member assignments, a struct copy (`v = data.offset`), temporaries and a `const &` all gave the same allocation. With temporaries, the dead store `v.y = y` (overwritten by `v.y = 0x1666`) was removed, which the target does not do. `func_ov002_021eca14` (6) is the same case: `v = p->c; self->b = 0x1f; self->w = v;` gives the target's order, but `v` gets its own register and `mov r0, #1` moves up. When only the order of independent instructions differs, look at which registers the values share first.
- **`byte store; load` stays in source order.** A `u8` store may alias any later load, so the scheduler never moves a load above it. If the target loads first, the load has to come first in the source (a local read before the store). An inline parameter does not help, because MWCC substitutes the argument expression where the parameter is used.
- **The switch-tree pivot and dummy labels.** In `func_ov008_021ce518` (1 diff) the inner switch has labels 11, 16, 20, 30 and 40. The target's root is `cmp #0x14; bgt`, and 20 is tested again in the left subtree. A dummy `case 21:` merged into `default:` gives the right tree shape, but its root is `cmp #0x15` (the dummy becomes the pivot). Every single dummy in 0..63, every pair in 0..47, 21 plus one more label, 21 plus two labels in 22..29, and moving `case 21`/`default` around (or giving it its own `break`) did not give `#0x14`.
- **Hill-climbing pointer locals per call site.** In `func_ov006_021de6bc` (draft) the `Model_PlayPart` inlines in the `unk34` block had the model and `res` registers swapped. A script tried, for each call site, the plain argument, `nv = arg`, `nv2 = arg`, `nv3 = arg` (function-level `ov006_Model *` locals) or the block's `m` local. It took the draft from 127 to 16 seqdiff lines (`ovlo12/nvs4.py`). The order of the inline's locals made no difference at all: 12 variants per site, 100 rounds, no change.
- **Two `if (t)` tests give `strne` but keep a second `cmp`.** `func_ov003_021ead50` (2) needs `cmp r5, #0; strne; beq`. `if (t) self->a = t; if (t && !t->flag) ...` gives the `strne`, followed by a redundant `cmp r5, #0`. Every nested, `else return`, `goto` and comma form put the store after the `beq`.
- **A loop-invariant written by hand is allocated last, and one LICM hoists is allocated first.** In `func_ov002_021e0ff8` (2) the target has `mov i, #0` before `add yy, y, #0x14`. Writing `y + 0x14 + ...` inside the loop (no `yy`) gives that order, because LICM hoists it after `i = 0`. But the hoisted temporary then takes `r4`, the lowest callee-saved register, and no declaration order fixes that (best 16). A hand-written `yy` gets the target's register, `r8`, but is scheduled before `i = 0`.
## Shared statement tails, retested conditions and loop-invariant flags (ov000 high and overlays 9–15, round big6)

- **An inline for a statement group that ends three paths gets them tail-merged.** In `func_ov000_021b2f08` (draft `scratchpad/big6/2f08/l.cpp`) three branches end with the same `q.unk_20 = lo; q.flags |= 8; q.unk_24 = hi;` and the target shares that tail (`rsb; str; orr; str` reached by a `b` from the other paths). Writing the stores directly, in any order, kept three copies. A `static inline void SetRange(fq, lo, hi)` used at every site merged them (structural diff 100 to 57). The target also makes `-0x2800`/`-0xf000` with `mov; rsb`: MWCC builds negative constants that way when the positive one is encodable, so `lo` can be a plain negative literal.
- **Branches with the same body: one `||` condition, or `else if` with duplicated bodies.** Separate `if (k == 5) { res = x; break; } if (bit && (...)) { res = x; break; }` blocks each got their own `ldr; b`. One `if (k == 5 || (bit && (k == 0xd || k == 0xf || k == 0x10)) || (!bit && (k == 7 || k == 8)))` gave the target's single target block and its compare chain. Inside that chain `k == 7 || k == 8` became the target's `add #0xf9; and #0xff; cmp #1` range check. A lone `if ((A) || kk == 2 || kk == 3) {X} else {Y}` did not give the target's `b` to a shared block. `if (A) {X} else if (kk == 2 || kk == 3) {X} else {Y}` did, because the two copies of `X` are tail-merged.
- **`h = C; if (bit) h = 0;` instead of `bit ? 0 : C`.** The target loads `-0x533` unconditionally and overwrites it with `movne rX, rZero`, reusing a zero register. The ternary gave `ldreq` (121 to 99 aligned in `021b2f08`).
- **`list[cnt] = x; cnt++;` against `list[cnt++] = x;`.** The post-increment form copied the old index into a scratch register first (`mov r2, sb; add sb, sb, #1; str r1, [r0, r2, lsl #2]`). The target stores, then increments.
- **A pointer local for the timer tick only.** `func_ov011_02204428` (draft `scratchpad/big6/4428/e.cpp`, 6 aligned diffs) is a state machine that does `if (t != 0) t--; if (t == 0) {...}` on `self->unk_40->timer` in every case. The target reuses the `unk_40` it loaded for the `switch` in those tick lines, but reloads it for every later write. `Ov011Sub40 *s = self->unk_40; switch (s->state)` with `s->timer` in the tick lines only, and `self->unk_40->...` everywhere else, reproduced both (28 to 13). A `permblk.py` run on one case's stores then put `Ov011_VecSet(&self->unk_84, ...)` before `self->unk_9c = 0x29;` (13 to 6). The last 6 diffs are the operand registers of `FX_Atan2(a38.x - a50.x, a38.z - a50.z)` on two globals. The target computes the x term first. Temporaries in either order, pointer locals, an inline helper, `volatile`/`const` externs and 1450 permuter iterations all gave the z term first.
- **`ok = A(); if (ok) ok = B(); if (ok) {...}` against `if (A() && B())`.** `func_ov011_021ff24c` (draft `scratchpad/big6/f24c/h.cpp`, 106 aligned diffs) materialises both results into one register and tests it again at the join (`beq L; ... L: cmp r2, #0`). `&&` branches straight out instead.
- **Inverting an `if`/`else` chain moves whole blocks.** In the same function the target places the "reset" block (`SetState(5)`) before the `cnt < 2` flag block. Writing the condition as `if (CountBusy() == 0 || (cnt == 0 && CountBusy() != 5)) {reset} else if (cnt < 2) ...` instead of the positive form fixed the block order (structural diff 67 to 30).
- **A materialised `!flag` as a loop invariant.** The ring-buffer size check in `021ff24c` keeps two invariants: `lsrs r6` (the valid bit) and `moveq r8, #1; movne r8, r7` (its negation). The negation only appeared with `BOOL e = r->f.b0 ? FALSE : TRUE; if (e) return 0; return r->cap;` in the capacity inline. `if (!r->f.b0) return 0;` tested the bit directly.
- **Permuter on ov011 C++.** After `setup.sh`, run `fixtd.py` (scratchpad `big4/`) on `base.c` and rewrite the `Ov011V` virtual calls as `VC_0c(e, x)`, `VC_08(self)` (wrap.py turns those back into `vfunc_XX` calls). Otherwise every compile fails and the log only says "Unable to compile".
- **`func_ov000_021ad8ac`, still 6.** Changing the declared types of `func_01ffc128`'s parameters, seven `static inline` wrappers around the arm call, and argument locals (including `u16`/`u32` types) all left the order of `mov r0, #imm` and `str sl, [sp]` alone. Two random searches (800 variants of per-arm statement order, argument locals, a result temporary and a local for the `0x9a0` read) found nothing below 6. One observation for a later attempt: with the call written once after the `if` chain, with per-arm variables for its arguments, the constant `tgt` load comes right after `add r2, r4, #0x7b` in the `0x40` arm, as in the target. The variables' copies are emitted in source order at the end of each arm. But the call setup moves into the join block (79 aligned).
## Statement position, hand-written asm in gaps and dead-stripped tables (main, round mainbig5)

- **Move an inlined multiply to a later statement.** `func_0205cbf0` (stage command, 6 aligned diffs for three rounds) differed only in the registers of an inlined `(s64)a * b + 0x800 >> 12`: the target put the operand `hh` in `r4` and the 64-bit result in `r3`/`r2`. Declaration order, `long`/`unsigned long` types, expression forms and `s64` temporaries changed nothing. Writing `s32 h;` without an initialiser and computing `h = StageFx_Mul(hh, 0xaab);` *after* the three `pos.x = 0; pos.y = 0; pos.z = 0;` stores matched it. The generated code is in the same place; only the register colouring changed. `scratchpad/mainbig5/mover.py src func 'statement'` moves one statement line to every other slot in the function and compiles each (`automover.sh` does it for every statement). Try it on any register-only near-miss before a permuter run. It did not help `func_02056e74`, `func_02039844`, `func_0206e3a0`, `func_0205c234` or `func_02050ebc`.
- **Duplicate a trailing assignment into both arms of an `if`.** `func_02068544` (effect object chain, 10 aligned diffs, all one register swap between the new object and the previous one) matched when `if (prev != NULL) { prev->parent = o; o->child = prev; } prev = o;` became `if (prev != NULL) { prev->parent = o; o->child = prev; prev = o; } else { prev = o; }`. `automover.sh` pointed at it: moving `prev = o;` into the `if` (which is wrong code) gave 1 diff, and the `else` copy made it correct.
- **Hand-written asm left in gaps.** `func_02032f90` (Mobiclip YUV to RGB conversion, which fills its whole gap) matched as an `asm void` function converted from the dsd disassembly: `ip` becomes `r12`, `push`/`pop` become `stmfd sp!`/`ldmfd sp!`, and `.L_x` labels become `@Lx`. dsd prints a single-register `str r10, [sp, #-4]!` as `push {r10}` and `ldr r10, [sp], #4` as `pop {r10}`. `stmfd sp!, {r10}` assembles to a different word, so write the `str`/`ldr` form.
- **Gaps that hold only data.** These caused two problems with the other Mobiclip asm, so it stays in gaps. `func_02077118` (the audio synthesis filter) matches as asm, with explicit `ldr rX, [pc, #-N]` for the pool words just before it; the draft is `scratchpad/mainbig5/mobiclip_asm_02077118.c`.
  - The linker runs with `-dead` and strips a gap object whose symbols nothing references. Everything after it then shifts, and `check_symbols` reports the next functions "found at" lower addresses. Splitting `func_0207670c` (a 4-byte `b func_02077118`) or `func_02076f3c` out of their gaps would leave gaps that hold only the tables the asm reads pc-relatively.
  - `func_02077118` is the last code in main's `.text`. Delinking it leaves a final gap with no `$a` mapping symbol. The build passes, but `ninja report` panics in objdiff (`arch/arm.rs:191`, index out of bounds). Before splitting a function out of a gap, check that what is left still has a referenced symbol and some code.
- **Not solved this round.**
  - `func_02058d10` (data resource singleton, new draft `scratchpad/mainbig5/d8d10.cpp`, 9 aligned diffs). The target loads the second string address (`ldr r7, =data_0208bdec`) before the first `func_0206e900` call and keeps it in a callee-saved register. Inline parameters, per-branch locals, `char *` types, null checks inside the inline and a nested inline all rematerialise it after the call. A local assigned before the `if` keeps it in `r7`, but loads it before the branch (4 aligned).
  - `func_02050ebc` (2). The target reads `owner->sub.id` as `add r0, r4, #0x3c0; ldrh r0, [r0, #0x12]`. Every form we tried (member and base-class inlines, casts, array indexing, a block-scoped `&owner->sub`) folds to `add #0x300; ldrh [#0xd2]`. A function-level `NameOwnerSub *sub` keeps the separate add but moves `name` too.
  - `func_0204ffdc` (52). With a variable `dy`, the target's `add r2, sb, r0` has the zero register as the first operand, so it cannot become an immediate. Every form we tried puts `dy` second and gives `add #0`. Extra `dy = 0;` statements at each of 86 positions changed nothing.

## Statement order is not load order, and what did not move (ov000, round lo14)

- **MWCC moves single-use locals to their use.** In `func_ov000_02166180` (draft `scratchpad/ov000lo14/6180_e.cpp`, 393 aligned diffs) the target loads the three `DirAttr` bitfields (`hf`, `vf`, `idx`) before the stores to the OAM entry. Reading them into three `u16` locals before the stores did not help: MWCC put each `ldrh` back after the stores, next to its use. A random search over all 720 orders of the tail statements also changed nothing. So when the target computes a value early, a local that is used only once will not force it there. The value has to be used twice, or come from a struct copy. Here, a `DirAttr` struct copy is placed on the stack (`strh [sp, #0x2a]`), which the target does not do.
- **`(x & 7) * 4` folds to `lsl #29; lsr #27`.** The target keeps `sec & 7` in a register (`and r1, r0, #7`) and shifts it again at each use. `s32`, `u32` and `u8` for `sec`, and `sec &= 7` as a separate statement, all gave the folded form. `u16`/`s16` gave `and #7` followed by `lsl #16; lsr #14`. This is unsolved.
- **Hoisting every block-scoped local to function scope and climbing the order** (45 declarations, 1500 steps) took `02166180` only from 412 to 393. This was the big5 recipe. Here it did not reach the main problem: the target keeps `ex`/`ey` in `r6`/`r7` and spills `ex - 4` in the second loop. Our build keeps them in `ip`/`r2` and spills the `oams` pointer instead. A large register-pressure difference like this one comes from structure, not from declaration order.
- **In `0218e12c`, callee-saved registers follow the order in which the variables are created.** Naming the destructor's loop variable (`while ((g = head) != NULL)`) gave it `r8`. That is after the group-level `prev`/`next` (`r6`/`r7`) and before the actor-level pair, which moved to `sb`/`sl`. The target gives the actor pair `r4`/`r5` and `saved`/`mgr` `r8`/`sb`, as if the innermost inline's locals were created first. None of these changed the order: `delete` on a group class with an inline destructor (same code as the `if` + inline), the whole body as a `static inline`, a `HeapState *` local, `void *saved`, or reordering the locals of the inlined removal.
- **Scheduling-only near-misses that random order search did not fix:** `0218af9c` (2; spill store of `on` before the `lsl`), with all 720 orders of the setup statements, a declaration climb and 20 minutes of the permuter. `02181c4c` (3; byte-store order of `id`/`c`/`d`), with all 720 orders of the `else` block, every order and inline variant of the three `Get2` calls, and explicit byte loads with all 720 store orders (best 11). `02189b44` (5; `r2`/`r3` swapped for `self->pos.x`/`.z`), with 16 spellings: locals, operand order, an argument-swapping `FX_Atan2` wrapper and shifts.

## Wider types for case locals, and early-return `BOOL` inlines (overlays 9–15 and ov000 high, round ovhi15)

- **`long` on case-block locals swaps two callee-saved registers.** In `func_ov011_021fbe08` case 1 copies an actor's `x`, `y`, `z` into three locals and writes them (`y + 0x6800`) into every node of a list. The target had `x` and `y + 0x6800` in `r6`/`r7`, the draft in `r7`/`r6` (4 diffs). Declaration order, load order, hoisting the locals to function scope with a hill climb, and pointer locals all changed nothing. Changing the types did: `y` and `z` must both be `long`, `u32` or `unsigned long` (the type of `x` does not matter), and `s32 x; long y; long z;` matches. This is the round mainbig4 `unsigned long` trick, but for registers rather than stack slots. A small script that tries `long`/`unsigned long`/`u32`/`s32` on each scalar local of the function and its inlines, one at a time and kept greedily, is cheap (`scratchpad/ovhi15/t/ts.py`). Across 55 other drafts in these overlays it found nothing more, so it is a quick check rather than a cure.
- **Write a game-state `BOOL` inline with early returns.** `func_ov000_021a2d64` tests "game idle" through a nested inline that the draft wrote as `BOOL r = FALSE; if (!Busy(g)) r = (g->state == 3); return r;`. That gave `ldreq; moveq r8, #0; cmpeq` (5 diffs). The target, `mov r8, #0; ... cmp r0, #0; bne; ldr; cmp r0, #3; moveq r8, #1`, came from `if (Busy(g)) return FALSE; if (g->state != 3) return FALSE; return TRUE;`. This is the form of `Ov014Game_IsIdle` in `include/ov014/ov014.h`, so reuse that shape whenever a draft inlines the same `0x90 != 0x94` / `state == 3` check. Early-return forms did not help `func_ov015_02200d50` or the `Is6`/`Is8`/`Is5` inlines of `func_ov010_0220a374`.
- **Two `ldrh`, two `strh`, one `ldrh`, one `strh` is a 6-byte struct copy.** For `func_ov010_021ffd0c` (13), a struct-typed global (`struct T { u8 pad[0x3c]; R3 r; }`, declared in a namespace with `extern "C"`) with `R3 r = tbl.r;` gives MWCC's pairwise copy order, which the target uses. A `FLD(...)` cast or element-wise copies do not. That draft is still 15, because the copy of the 12-byte vector is scheduled differently.
- **Still open:** `func_ov013_022001b8` (3) needs `movne r3, #0; moveq r3, #1` for a `x == 1` argument. Bool and ternary forms, enum ternaries, early-return and switch inlines all give `mov #1; movne #0` or `mov #0; moveq #1`. In matched code that pair only shows up when the `== k` test is the last term of an `&&` chain inside an inline. `func_ov000_0219badc` (5) is the redundant-`cmp` case after `strne` from round ovlo12. Nested `if`, `else return`, early return and a repeated `tbl != NULL` all give the same code.

## Store-order search on a query block, merged count variables and argument order at -O2 (ov000 high and overlays 9–15, round big7)

- **Search the order of a query struct's setup stores.** In `func_ov000_021b2f08` (draft `scratchpad/big7/2f08/p.cpp`) the stores that fill the finder query before the first `func_ov000_02199554` call (`flags |= 6`, `unk_0c`, `pos.x/y/z`, `unk_1c`, `unk_18 = 0`, `finder.query = NULL`) were in the wrong order, and the target keeps `flags | 6` in `fp` until after the word stores. A random shuffle of those eight lines (`ovlo12/rs.py`) found `pos.x; finder.query = NULL; unk_0c; pos.y; pos.z; unk_1c; flags |= 6; unk_18 = 0;` in under 100 tries. The register-masked structural diff went from 20 to 0 (98 to 94 aligned). The byte store `flags |= 6` had to come after `unk_1c`, as the round ovlo12 note on byte stores predicts.
- **One count variable for two finder calls.** The same function has three phases, each with its own result count (`n`, `n2`, `n3`). Reusing `n2` for the third call's result took it from 94 to 85. The remaining 85 are a consistent `r8`/`sb` swap through the whole function (the zero/`res` register and the `r` radius), plus the spill slots of `y` and `s`. Declaration-order climbs (21 locals, two runs), `long`/`u32`/`int` types for every scalar local, `res` initialised at five different places and a 25-minute permuter run did not change them.
- **At -O2 and above, the `FX_Atan2` argument code did not change with the source.** In `func_ov011_02204428` (still 6) a standalone test file (`scratchpad/big7/cdis.py` compiles a snippet and disassembles one function) shows that at `-O1` MWCC computes the x argument first and the order of `dx`/`dz` temporaries is kept. At `-O2` to `-O4` the code for `FX_Atan2(A.x - B.x, A.z - B.z)` is identical for temporaries in either order, block or function-scope temporaries, struct differences, inline wrappers with swapped parameters, casts (`s64`, `u32`, `long long`), no-op operators (`|0`, `*1`, `<<0`, `~~`, `-(-x)`), pointer locals for either vector and `#pragma opt_propagation off`/`opt_common_subs off`. The target's allocation (`&A` in `r2`, `B.x` in `r0` as the destination of `sub r0, r3, r0`) means `&A` stays live across both `B` loads. `s32 dx = B.x; dx = A.x - dx;` was the only form that changed which operand shares the result register in the test file, but in the real function it made things worse (6 to 7).
- **A `u32` parameter for an `e == 2 || e == 3` inline changed more than the compare.** In `func_ov011_021ff24c` (draft `scratchpad/big7/f24c/j.cpp`) changing `F24c_IsMove(s32 e)` to `u32 e` took 106 to 84 aligned. The compare itself still becomes `sub #2; cmp #1` where the target has `cmp #2; beq; cmp #3; movne`; switch, ternary, separate `if`s and narrower types all gave 107–111. Computing the ring index in its own `static inline u16 F24c_Idx(...)` with an `if`/`return` gave 83.
## String literals blocked by big pools, stores in switch arms (main, round mainbig6)

- **A string address kept in a saved register means a literal, even in main.** `func_0204c158` (message resource singleton, 5 aligned diffs) and `func_02058d10` (data resource singleton, 9) load the second file-name argument (`ldr r7, =str`) before the first `func_0206e900` call and pass it with `mov r1, r7` later. Both match at 0 with real literals: `MsgRes_OpenFile2(res, "script/cmninfo", "cmn.ccmes")` and `DataRes_Open1530("script/cmninfo", "str.ccstr")`. `extern const char[]`, the `namespace K { extern "C" const char x[]; }` form from the `const` tip, and an integer address cast `(const char *)0x0208b9dc` all rematerialise the address after the call instead. Neither function can be merged yet, because `tools/strrefs.py` shows each pool running far past the function. `0x0208b9dc` continues with the colour names used by `func_0204d040` and the `s00`/`s01` strings of `func_02052628`/`func_02052bec`. `0x0208bdec` continues with an unreferenced `"%s_%.3d"`, then `"flg.ccflg"` (`func_02058f2c`, which has the same pattern) and the `.ccmes`/`*Tbl` names up to `func_02062138`. A file that claims one of those pools has to cover all of that code. Drafts: `scratchpad/mainbig6/d/c158.cpp` and `d8d10.cpp`; swap in the literals once the surrounding files can be merged.
- **Assign in every `switch` arm to keep a byte store above the next loads.** In `func_0206e858` (language setup) the target stores the language byte before it loads `*name` and the destination for the next call. With `st->lang = Lang_FromOwner();` (the inline returns from a switch), MWCC scheduled both loads above the `strb` in the join block, because it knows the store and the loads hit different globals. With `st->lang = k;` written in each `case` and `default`, the arms are tail-merged into one `mov r0, #k` per arm plus one `strb r0, [r5]` at the join, as in the target. The store then stays first and the value is in `r0`. Assigning `st = &data_0214b104;` before `name = &data_0208cb88;` gave the target's pool order. This took the draft from 9 diffs to 1 (`scratchpad/mainbig6/d/e858_best.cpp`). The last diff: the target loads the same destination (`data_0214b105`) from the pool for both calls. If both calls name the same symbol, in any spelling, MWCC keeps it in the free `r5`. Two different symbols give separate pool words, and `st->name` gives `add r0, r5, #1`.
- **One register rotation in three VRAM-free loops.** `func_0204f524` (12), `func_0205592c` (11) and `func_02051210` (10) differ only in where the loop counter goes. Each frees VRAM blocks in a loop through the inline `MIi_CpuClearFast(0, (u8 *)(b->kind == 4 ? 0x6400000 : 0x6600000) + b->addr, ...)`. The target gives `i` the lowest free callee-saved register, before the two hoisted constants. We give it to the constants first. In `func_02051210` the target even moves the two `mov #0x6x00000` into the entry block, above the heap-swap `delete` code. Nothing below changed a single diff in any of the three:
  - loop counter types (`long`, `unsigned long`, `u32`, `u16`, `u8`), `register`, a `do`/`while` loop, or a block-scoped or C++ `for (int k ...)` counter;
  - `if`/`else` or pointer forms of the base, nested inlines (`VramBase`/`VramAddr`), the base constants as locals in all 24 declaration and assignment orders (they are constant-propagated), or a direct body instead of the `FreeBlocks` inline;
  - `automover.sh` and 400 iterations of the random order search.
  A fix for one should fix all three, so try new ideas on `592c.c`, the smallest (a permuter setup is in `scratchpad/mainbig6/p592c`).
- **The search tools this round.** `scratchpad/mainbig6/rso.py src func module iters` is a random hill climb over statement order. It looks at runs of single-line statements and declarations at the same indent and tries swaps, moves and shuffles, keeping any result that is no worse. Together with `automover.sh`, it ran on `func_0205c234`, `func_0206e3a0`, `func_02050ebc`, `func_02039844`, `func_0206e858`, `func_0204f524`, `func_020508f4`, `func_02051210` and `func_0205592c`. It found nothing that is valid code. The one hit, `func_0206e858` at 3, came from using `st` before it was assigned, but it showed that an unknown store pointer keeps the `strb` in place, and that led to the switch-arm fix above. Neither tool can reorder code inside a `static inline`.
## ITCM hit-mesh triangle tests (itcm, round itcm1)

- **What the four ITCM gap functions are.** They are the per-triangle collision tests behind `HitMeshItcm.cpp` and `HitRayItcm.cpp`, all compiled C (MWCC spills, `smull`/`smlal` fixed-point inlines). `func_01ffae08` casts a ray against every block of an unsorted mesh (it is `func_01ffb83c`'s twin). `func_01ff868c` does the same against a quadtree. It walks a 256-entry node stack on the frame (`sub sp, #0x400`), and its leaves hold `HitTri *` arrays. `func_01ff9f9c` tests a sphere against one triangle (called per triangle from `func_01ffb9b0`), and `func_01ff9060` is the quadtree version. Each triangle is 0x4c bytes: an `s16` bbox, an attribute mask at 0x0c, the plane distance at 0x10, three `s16` vertices at 0x14, the `s16` normal at 0x26 and three `s16` edge vectors at 0x2c. A hit fills one of 24 `0x3c`-byte records in `data_02fe0f50`, or `data_02fe14b4` once all 24 are used, and keeps the nearest in `data_02fe0ef8`.
- **The ray test body is shared code.** The two ray functions contain the same per-triangle code with different registers. Matching one gives the other, so build both from one body. The drafts are `scratchpad/itcm1/ae08.cpp` and `scratchpad/itcm1/hittri.cpp`; `gen868.py` makes the quadtree version from the `ae08` body.
- **Attribute filters are `if (cond) goto ok; goto fail;`.** The target's `tst; bne ok; b fail` for each of modes 0–2 (and `teq; beq fail` for mode 3, where `ok` is the next block) came from writing every case as `if ((mask & attr) ...) goto ok; goto fail;` in an `if`/`else if` chain. `if (!(...)) goto fail;` gave `beq fail`. A `BOOL` inline gave `moveq` materialisation. Mode 3 is `(mask ^ attr) != 0`, which gives `teq`; `mask != attr` gives `cmp`.
- **Mixed `smull` and `umull; mla; mla` in one function means `s64` locals.** The plane distance from `to` uses `smull/smlal` with the `s16` normal. The distances from `from` and `dir` use `umull` plus two `mla` with spilled `asr #31` high words, because there the normal components are `s64` locals (`s64 nx = tri->n[0];`). The declaration order `ny, nx, nz` gave the target's spill slots.
- **A `p - v` edge vector through an inline with pointer parameters.** The target copies `p.x` into a second register (`mov r1, r0`) before the first edge's `sub`. `e.x = p.x - tri->v[i][0];` written out never did this. `static inline void HitVecSubS16(const VecFx32 *a, const s16 *b, VecFx32 *out)` did (20 to 13 aligned diffs). Read the inline as being in the original source.
- **3x3 multiplies read their input into three locals first.** The target loads the normal (or vector) components once and then writes the three rows to the same stack vector. `fx32 x = v->x, y = v->y, z = v->z;` at the top of an inline (`HitMulVec33(&e, &m, &e)`) reproduces it. Without the locals, MWCC reloads after each row store.
- **Not solved this round.**
  - `func_01ffae08` (13 aligned diffs, `scratchpad/itcm1/ae08.cpp`) and `func_01ff868c` (36, `scratchpad/itcm1/hittri.cpp`). Both have the same two schedule differences. (1) The hit position is scaled by the owner scale (`x` stored, then `y`/`z`). The target stores `y` (`r8`) and then `z` (`r2`). Every order of temporaries and stores (216 variants), `s64` temporaries, inline helpers and `VecSet`-style argument lists gave either `z` before `y` or an `stmib` with the registers swapped. (2) In the `origin - pos` distance, the target loads `info->pos.y`/`.z` above the stores to the stack vector, and ours does not. Separate locals, inlines and `HitMagSq` changed nothing. A separate non-escaping vector gets scalarised and its stores disappear. `868c` also allocates the popped node to `r2` where the target uses `r1` (the target then reloads `top` from its stack slot). Pop spellings, push/pop inlines and the type of `top` did not change that. Declaration hill climbs on both functions and 339 permuter iterations on `ae08` found nothing either. Making the whole triangle test one `static inline` with `return`s made it much worse (400).
  - `func_01ff9f9c` / `func_01ff9060` (sphere tests) were not drafted. Their fixed-point code differs from the ray pair. The `+ 0x800` is materialised (`mov rX, #0x800; adds; adc rY, rZ` with a zero register), which suggests a different `FX_Mul` spelling (an `s64` constant). Each of the three `-5` edge thresholds also has its own stack slot, so the edge tests are probably separate inline calls. After the plane test they find the closest point on each edge, using `VEC_Fx16DotProduct` and `FX_Sqrt`, and then on each vertex.
- **Permuter on ITCM.** `tools/permuter/mktarget.py` and `setup.sh` handle overlays only. `scratchpad/itcm1/ptools` is a copy that reads `extract/usa/arm9/itcm.bin` and names the function `func_<addr>`, and `scratchpad/itcm1/prun.sh` runs it. Set `TMPDIR` to the scratch dir, because `cc.sh` writes temporary files there. It managed about 15 iterations a minute on these 2.5 KB functions while other workers were running, which is too slow to help much.

## Main string pools `0x0208b9dc` and `0x0208bdec` (round mainpool1)

- **A pool cannot be split between files.** `dsd`'s linker script starts the `.arm9` section with `ALIGNALL(4)`, and so did the original link: every input section starts on a 4-byte boundary. A string that starts right after the previous one's terminator, at an address that isn't a multiple of 4, is therefore in the same object as the string before it. Neither pool has such a gap: `"script/cmninfo"` (`func_0204c158`) ends at `0x0208b9f5` and `func_0204d040`'s first colour name starts there, and `"script/cmninfo"` (`func_02058d10`) ends at `0x0208be05`, directly followed by the unreferenced string and then `func_02058f2c`'s `"flg.ccflg"`. So each pool is one translation unit (TU). These workarounds were tested and fail:
  - `#pragma pool_strings off`: each literal gets its own `.data` section (`@NNN`), and `ALIGNALL(4)` aligns each one (the MSL `printf.c` strings show this in the target). The function itself still matches, so literal semantics don't depend on pooling.
  - Literals for the first function plus named global arrays (`char data_0208b9f5[] = "WHITE";`) for the rest of the pool: MWCC writes a TU's `.data` as globals in definition order, then vtables, then `@stringBase0` last. The named arrays would come before the pool, not after it.
  - The pool symbol is local, so another object can't reference a string inside it.
- **What decides the pool contents.** `@stringBase0` is always the last thing in a TU's `.data`. Its strings are in first-use order, and that includes strings in data initializers. A string from a `static` function that is never called stays in the pool even after the linker dead-strips the function. That is probably where the unreferenced string at `0x0208be05` comes from. Strings in an uncalled `static inline` function, or under `if (0)`, are not pooled.
- **Literal-blocked functions, verified at 0 diffs with literals.** `func_0204c158` (`scratchpad/mainpool1/d/c158_lit.cpp`), `func_02058d10` and `func_02058f2c` (both in `d8d10_f2c_lit.cpp`). `func_02058f2c` is the `flg.ccflg` twin of `func_02058d10`: `DataRes_New(0x50, data_0208be94)` into `data_02fe152c`, then `DataRes_Open152c("script/cmninfo", "flg.ccflg")`. With `extern` strings it is 21 diffs. The other functions that load these pools (`func_0204d040`, `func_02052628`, `func_02052bec`, `func_0205a840`, `func_02061d64`..`func_02062138`) already match with `extern` strings.
- **Plan: merge each span into one TU.** The minimal spans below run from the first to the last function that loads the pool, extended to whole existing files. The real TU may be larger; the 4 pointer tables at `0x0208b9cc` just before pool 1 may also belong to it.
  - **TU1, pool `0x0208b9dc`..`0x0208ba20`:** `.text` `0x0204c158`..`0x02052fcc`. It has 103 functions: the 20 files `MsgRes_3`, `TextWin`, `TextWinScan`, `TextWin_2`..`TextWin_13`, `TextWinOam`, `SlideWin`, `Obj3D`, `SceneShake`, `Obj3D_2`, plus the unmatched functions. `func_0204c158` matches with literals, `func_0204c4fc` is matched (round mainpool3) `func_0204d5bc` and `func_0204f804` are matched (round mainpool5) and `func_02050ebc` is matched (round mainpool6), and `func_02051210` is matched (round mainpool8, now at the start of `SlideWin.cpp`), so 6 block the merge: `0204e5d4` (40 aligned), `0204f524` (3, `scratchpad/mainpool8/d/f524_3.c`), `0204f5bc` (1, `scratchpad/mainpool8/d/f5bc_1.cpp`), `0204ffdc` (52, with `scratchpad/mainbig4/textwin.patch`), `020508f4` (8) and `02051a34` (35), about 5.5 KB. The counts are `match.py` differing instructions; see the four "TU1 blockers" sections below for the drafts.
  - **TU2, pool `0x0208bdec`..`0x0208be8c`:** `.text` `0x02058d10`..`0x02062844`. It has 246 functions: `DataRes_2`..`_6`, `ObjPool`, `ObjPool_2`, `StageRes`..`_5`, `StageCmd_0`, `StageCmd`, `StageCmd_2`..`_13` (26 files), plus 12 unmatched functions. `func_02058d10` and `func_02058f2c` match with literals, so 9 are left: `02059c98`, `0205a358`, `0205b1b4` (2: the target puts the function pointer for `data_0208bde8` in `r2` and the zero in `r1`), `0205b68c`, `0205c234`, `0205e29c`, `0205e5c4` (25), `02060a08` and `02060b18`, about 4.4 KB. The pointer `data_0208bde8` (= `func_0205bef8`) sits just before the pool and is part of TU2's `.data`.
  - **When every blocker matches.** Make one source file per span (or one file that `#include`s the existing ones). Remove duplicate `static inline` and `extern` declarations. Write all literals as literals, add the uncalled `static` function for the unreferenced string, and give the file one `delinks.txt` entry. The entry covers the whole `.text` span, the TU's `.data` up to the pool's end (TU1: at least `0x0208b9dc`..`0x0208ba20`; TU2: from wherever its `.data` starts, through `0x0208be8c`), and the contiguous `.bss` of the merged files. Then run `strrefs.py --claim`. Until then, keep the three literal drafts in scratch.
- **`func_0205bec8` matched** (`StageCmd_0.cpp`): the callee and data are in overlay 8, and `relocs.txt` marks the call `overlays(2,8)`. `void func_0205bec8(u16 id) { func_ov008_021cf9b8(data_ov008_021d0500, id); }` matches as a tail call.
## TU1 blockers (main, round mainpool3)

- **A narrow parameter type can fix a prologue rotation.** `func_0204c4fc` (text measure, 7 diffs for several rounds) only differed in its entry block: the target loads the stack argument `raw` first and puts the `str` copy in `r4` and the `data_02097ce0` pointer in `r5`, where we swapped them and spilled `spacing` first. Declaring the last parameter `bool raw` (also `u8`, `char`, `u16` or `s16`) instead of `BOOL` gives exactly the target, although `raw` is only ever tested against zero and is still read with a word `ldr`. The callers keep their own `BOOL` prototypes, so nothing else changes. The permuter found it (it changed the type to `unsigned short`). When an entry block's spills and copies are scheduled differently, try the types of the stack parameters.
- **The `goto test` outer loop also fits `func_020508f4`.** The text box line splitter has the same outer loop as `func_0204c2f4`: `goto test; do { while (...) {...} test: if (*s == 0) break; } while (*s != 0);`. It adds the target's redundant `beq` before the back branch and took the draft from 28 to 8 raw diffs (`scratchpad/mainpool3/d/08f4_g8.cpp`). The 8 left are one swap: the target keeps `fn` in `r7` and `dst` in `r5`; we swap them. Declaration order (all 28 positions), `NNSi_G2dFontGetSpliter` and moving the `dst` assignment did not change it, and 400 iterations of `rso.py` found nothing.
- **`func_0204d5bc` (tag parser) is down to register swaps.** In `TextWin_CopyParen`, `*dst++ = s[j++ + 1];` gives the target's loop (`add r3, r2, r1; ldrsb ip, [r3, #1]; add r1, r1, #1; ...`). The separate `j++;` statement made MWCC keep a second copy of `j` and cost 60 diffs. Draft `scratchpad/mainpool3/d/d5bc_39.cpp` (39). What is left: `q` and `i` are swapped (`r8`/`sb`), and in both inlined copies `j` and `dst` are swapped (`r0`/`r1`). Declaration order, block-scoped `i`, `i`/`c` types, swapping the inline's parameter order, a `ParseOpts` inline for the `'o'` loop, `while ((q = ...) != NULL)`/`for` forms and writing `FindComma` out by hand did not change any of them.
- **The VRAM-free rotation, more data.** `func_0204f5bc` (12, `scratchpad/mainlow9/parked/TextWin_7_0204f5bc.cpp`) has the same loop and differs only in it, so one fix would match four functions (`0204f524`, `0204f5bc`, `02051210`, `0205592c`). In every form we compile, the hoisted constants are colored in reverse order of their materialization: in `kind == 4 ? 0x6400000 : 0x6600000` the zero gets `r4`, `0x6600000` `r5`, `0x6400000` `r6` and `i` the last register; writing `kind != 4 ? 0x6600000 : 0x6400000` swaps the two constants (and the `movne` becomes `moveq`). The target has zero, `i`, `0x6400000`, `0x6600000`, which looks like constants that were created before `i`. In `func_02051210` the target also materialises them in the entry block, above the `delete` code. None of these changed the allocation: `#pragma opt_loop_invariants off` (the constants are still hoisted, so the backend does it), `opt_strength_reduction`, `opt_lifetimes`, `opt_common_subs`, `optimize_for_size`, `optimization_level 3`/`2`, the SDK inlines `G2_GetOBJCharPtr`/`G2S_GetOBJCharPtr`, constants passed as inline arguments, `goto`/`while (1)` loops, `i` reused for another value before the loop, and `u8 *` locals or `static` constants for the bases. 40 minutes of the permuter on `0204f524` (`scratchpad/mainpool3/pf524`) found nothing.
- **`func_02050ebc` (2).** A second base class at `0x3c0` (`struct NameOwner : NameOwnerBase, NameOwnerSub`) does not help either: plain member access and reference casts fold to `#0x300 + #0xd2`, and pointer casts add a null check (30). A function-level `sub = &owner->sub;` keeps the `#0x3c0` add only when it is assigned in an earlier block (after the `func_0206ffa0` call: 6 diffs, but the add moves above the branch). In the same block as the use, it is propagated and folded.
- **Best drafts of the other blockers:** `0204e5d4` `scratchpad/mainlow23/sd/e5d4h.cpp`, `0204f524` `scratchpad/mainbig6/d/f524.c`, `0204ffdc` `scratchpad/mainbig4/dffdc_52.cpp` (with `textwin.patch`), `02050ebc` `scratchpad/mainbig4/func_02050ebc_TextWin11_2.cpp`, `02051210` `scratchpad/mainbig6/d/1210.cpp`, `02051a34` `scratchpad/mainbig4/func_02051a34_35.cpp`. `0204f804` (0x448 bytes) has no draft.
- **`func_02051a34` (35)** is one three-way rotation (`win`, `fn` and the hoisted `0xffff0000`). Making it a member function and the `goto test` loop (43) did not help.

## TU1 blockers, round 2 (main, round mainpool5)

- **`dst[0] = c; dst++;` is not `*dst++ = c;`.** `func_0204d5bc` (tag parser) was 39 diffs. The extended type scan (`scratchpad/ovhi16/t/ts.py`) found `long i` for the `'o'` loop index, which fixed the `q`/`i` swap (`r8`/`sb`) and left 20: in both inlined copies of `TextWin_CopyParen`, `j` and `dst` were in each other's scratch registers (`r0`/`r1`), and the target kept `j` apart from the return value (`add r0, r1, #1`). Writing the copy loop body as `dst[0] = s[j++ + 1]; dst++;` (and `dst[0] = 0;` after it) matched. The two forms give the same instructions, but in the target `dst` ends up coalesced with the return value instead of `j`. So when an inlined copy loop has a scratch-register swap between the index and the pointer, try splitting the post-increment store into a store and a separate increment. The same change on `func_020508f4`'s copy loop made it worse (13).
- **A new draft matched by climbing the declaration order: `func_0204f804`.** This 0x448-byte text typewriter step (one glyph per call into a VRAM block) had no draft. The first draft was 63 aligned diffs. The case order (`0x14`, `0x11`, `0x12`, `0x10`, from the jump-table targets), `TextWin_SetOrigin` for the two `GfxMgr` stores, `c = ' '` before `pad = 8`, reading `data_02097ce0->unk_00c` back before the `numBlocks` increment, and the 24 orders of the 4 end-of-page statements (`page++; unk_3d8++; flag25 = 1; unk_18 = 0;`) took it to 19. What was left was a stack-slot swap (`font`/`pad`) and a register swap (`fn`/`stop`). The type scan found nothing. `hc.py` (hill climb over the lines of a block, `scratchpad/ovhi16/t/hc.py`) on the 9 declarations found an order with 0 diffs in under 5 minutes. Moving one declaration at a time had only reached 19, so let the climb swap pairs too.
- **The same climb found nothing for the other blockers.** It ran on the declarations of `020508f4` (14 lines), `02051a34` (11), `0204ffdc` (12, with `textwin.patch`) and `0204e5d4` (31), 120–150 rounds each. The type scan improved `0204e5d4` from 57 to 40 (`s16 sh`, which holds `bp & 0x1f`, and `long outline`; draft `scratchpad/mainpool5/d/e5d4_40.cpp`). What is left there is stack-slot assignment and a `bp`/`w`/`sh` rotation in the pixel loop. Block-scoped `w`, `sh` and `t` did not change it.
- **`func_0204ffdc` (52): the target's zero register is not a constant operand.** Besides the `r7`/`sb` swap of the zero register and the line pointer, the target computes `y` as `add r2, sb, r0` (zero register plus the offset), where we fold `dy = 0` into `add r2, r0, #0`. Writing `dy` first in the sum folds it away completely (58). A second `dy = 0` in the loop changes nothing. Something keeps the target's `dy` from constant propagation. Finding that may also fix the swap.
- **No new leads on the VRAM-free rotation (`0204f524`, `0204f5bc`, `02051210`).** If/else and `switch` forms for the base, a `VramBase` inline (ternary, early return, `int kind` parameter, `void *` result), the sum written in other orders, `win->numBlocks = i = 0`, `for (win->numBlocks = i = 0; ...)`, a `do { } while (0)` around the loop and an extra earlier function using the same inline all gave 12 (10 in `02051210`). Every compiler version gives the same 12. In `02051210` the target materialises both constants in the entry block, interleaved with the first loads, so they come from something above the `delete` block. Locals initialised at the top of the function are propagated and do not do it.
- **`func_02050ebc` (2) still folds `0x3c0 + 0x12` into `0x300 + 0xd2`.** These also fold: a member function on the sub-object, a second base class (with or without a vtable) and an inline member call through it, an inline returning `&o->sub`, `owner->sub->id` with a one-element array, a constant or runtime-zero index (`[n - 4]`), a volatile pointer cast, and `id` as a 16-bit bit-field. An inline that takes the sub pointer and the name gives 14.

## Sphere triangle tests and CSE of sign extensions (itcm, round itcm2)

- **Write every fixed-point product as `(s64)a * b`, with no `s64` locals, and let CSE choose `umull` or `smull`.** `func_01ff9f9c` (sphere against one triangle) mixes the two forms in a way that looks random. The projection `p = center - dist * n` uses `umull; mla; mla` for x and `smull` for y and z. The first edge dot uses `smull`, and the second and third edges use `umull` with the high words of `n.y`/`n.z` spilled. A small test (`scratchpad/itcm2/x4.cpp`, `x6.cpp`) shows that plain casts give exactly this. MWCC turns the sign extension of the first plane-dot term (`n.x`) into a 64-bit common subexpression, and that CSE then needs the full multiply. Each later repeat of a cast that has already been seen also uses the full multiply. `s64 nx = tri->n[0];` locals (the ray functions' trick) turn every projection and edge product into `umull` (only the plane dot keeps `smull`), so do not use them here.
- **An address-taken stack vector can get a second, scalar copy.** In the sphere test the projected point is stored twice per component (`str [sp, #0x10]` and `str [sp, #0x10c]`). That is one variable `p`, later reused as the hit point and passed to `MTX_MultVec43`, not `hit = p`. A separate `hit` copy put the scalar copies somewhere else. The edge tests read `p` back from the low copy.
- **The info block differs between the sphere tests.** `func_01ff9f9c` reads the hit point into three locals before scaling it by `owner->scale` (all three `ldr`, then `str x, y, z`, like `HitMulVec33`). It keeps `HitTriOwner *owner = q->owner;` for both `distSq` and `MI_Copy48B`. `func_01ff9060` uses the ray functions' form instead.
- **Control flow in the closest-feature part.** Edge 0 writes its closest point straight into `p`. Edges 1 and 2 build a temporary and copy it when `found == 0 || s <= dist`. Edge 2 does not set the flag; it ends with `goto found`. The vertex tests run only if `found == 0`, and when vertex 2 is out of range the function returns, even if vertex 0 or 1 hit. Write it that way.
- **What did not change anything.** The order of the edge-local declarations and of the three cross-product lines (all 36 orders gave the same code: single-use locals move to their use). Ten spellings of the rounding constant (`0x800LL`, `(s64)0x800`, `(1LL << 11)`, `(u64)`, ...) all give `adds #0x800; adc #0` on their own. The target's `mov rX, #0x800` and zero register in `adc` come from constant CSE across the function, not from the macro.
- **Drafts.** `scratchpad/itcm2/9f9c.cpp` (9f9c: register-masked seqdiff 154, 922 instructions against 923) and `9060.cpp`, which `gen9060.py` builds from the 9f9c body inside the quadtree walk of `func_01ff868c` (masked 320). In 9f9c the target keeps the zero in `r3` from the entry, so most registers in the first half differ. The frame is 0x124 against 0x118.
- **`ae08`/`868c` (still 13 and 36 diffs).** None of these changed the hit-position store order: multiply-assigned function-scope temporaries (`f`/`t`) instead of fresh locals, `fx32 *` stores (`pos[0..2]`, `*pos++`), every `HitVecScale*` inline, `VecFx32 *pos` locals, reading `p` into locals first (49). Nor did these change the `origin - pos` loads: `HitDistSq`, `HitVecSub` + `HitDot`/`HitMagSq`, swapped term order or a `VecFx32 *pos` local. `mover.py` on the eight statements around both spots, the type scan with `fx32` added, and 25 minutes of the permuter (about 100 iterations a minute when nothing else runs) found nothing either.
- **Tool.** `scratchpad/itcm2/sd.py src func itcm -m` masks registers and stack offsets in the sequence diff. On a fresh draft of a large function it is a better guide to structure than the raw count, which register noise dominates (691 raw against 154 masked for 9f9c).

## Constructed locals, `.rodata` as `const` and moving a pointer into a loop (ov000, round big8)

These took the ov000 mini-map marker function `func_ov000_021675ec` (1912 bytes, `src/ov000/MiniMap_021675ec.cpp`) from 245 aligned diffs to a match. It had been on the ov000 skip lists since round lo5.

- **A local with a constructor stays on the stack.** The target stores the player's x/z into an 8-byte stack object once at the top. The first loop uses the stored registers, and the two later loops reload the values from the stack before the loop (`ldr r4, [sp, #0x18]; ldr fp, [sp, #0x1c]`). Plain struct locals were always split into registers, whatever accessed them: a pointer passed to an inline, a nested inline, an aggregate initialiser, a two-element array, a struct copy inside the inline or a by-value parameter (that added one copy per call). A struct-returning `MkPos()` kept it in memory, but with an extra 8-byte temporary. `struct Ctr : Pos { Ctr(s32 a, s32 b) { x = a; y = b; } };` declared as `Ctr c(pl->x, pl->z);` gave exactly the target's frame (136 to 50 aligned). If the frame is 8 or 12 bytes larger than the draft's and the target reloads a small vector from the stack, try a class with a constructor.
- **Mixed declarations set the slot order.** With `c` constructed in mid-function, the function-scope `Pos m;` took the higher slot. Declaring `Pos m;` right after `c` (C++ allows it) swapped the two slots, as in the target (50 to 16).
- **Index a struct array instead of `base + i * 4`.** `ents[i * 4]` and `*(u16 *)(ents + i * 4 + 2)` shared one address register. The target computes the address again after the branch, which only came from `struct { u8 kind; u8 pad; u16 id; } *ents` with `ents[i].kind` and `ents[i].id`.
- **`automover.sh` found the last register rotation.** Moving the loop-invariant `buf = (Oam *)(w + 0x64c);` from before the loop into the loop body (just before `u8 n = w[2]++;`) fixed the rotation of four callee-saved registers (21 to 2). Writing it as `&((Oam *)(w + 0x64c))[n]` does the same. Declaration-order climbs and the type scan had not changed it.
- **Declare `.rodata` symbols `const`.** The last 2 diffs were a struct copy from `data_ov000_021c9a9c + 0x74`: the target loads both words before either store, the draft interleaved them. That symbol lies in ov000's `.rodata`, and declaring it `extern const u8 data_ov000_021c9a9c[];` let the loads move above the stores. Check `delinks.txt` for the section ranges: any data symbol in `.rodata` can be `const`, and a load that the target hoists above an unrelated store is a sign of it. Statement orders, temporaries, pointer locals and inline setters did not reproduce it.
- **`G2_SetOBJShape`-style masks.** Setting two OAM bitfields as `e->shape = 0; e->size = 1;` gave `bic; bic; orr`. The target's `ldr =mask; and; orr #0x40000000` came from one word operation: `attr = (attr & ~0xc000c000) | 0x40000000`. An unsigned shift needs `(u32)(u16)x >> 12` (the round big5 note), or MWCC uses `asr`.
- **`func_ov000_02166be8`: a shared check as an inline made declaration order matter.** In the same class's update function (draft `scratchpad/big8/6be8/best29.cpp`) every diff was one permutation of callee-saved registers: the target colours the hoisted zero and `k` first (`r4`, `r5`) and `self` fifth (`r8`), the draft gave `self` `r4`. Declaration order (400 hill-climb steps), the type scan, a copy of `self`, the body as an inline, `const` on the table and 12 minutes of the permuter changed nothing at all. Writing the "paused" test that cases 4 and 7 both open with (`skip = FALSE; if (!player) skip = TRUE; if (Has(q, 9) || Has(q, 10)) skip = TRUE;`) as one `static inline BOOL Skip()` moved `self` to `r8`, and after that the declaration order did matter: a hill climb went from 109 to 40 aligned diffs. When declaration order changes nothing, look for a repeated block that should be an inline.
- **A struct-copy local declared after a statement.** Declaring `Pos c = table->pos;` after `tgt = ctx->target;` (a C++ mixed declaration) put the `ctx` load first at the top of the function, as in the target (40 to 33). Assigning `c` as a statement instead split it into registers.
- **A local for a field that is read and then updated keeps the high-word zero out of a register.** The tail computes `dx = ... - w->cbc` and later `w->cbc += FX_MUL(k, dx)`. The target loads `cbc` once and keeps it, and its two `+ 0x800` use `adc #0`. The draft reloaded `cbc` and kept the zero high word in a register (`mov r3, #0` hoisted, `adc ..., r3`). `ox = w->cbc;` used in both places fixed both (33 to 29, no structural diffs left). `w->cc0` is reloaded in the target, so it stays a field expression. The last 29 are the registers of the 64-bit tail (`e`, `dz` and the `sq` low word): moving each tail statement, the type scan, the `sq` operand order and four `FX_MUL` signatures did not fix them.
- **Quick checks on the other big near-misses this round.** `automover.sh` is slow on these (one to three minutes per statement), so it ran fully only on `021675ec`. On `func_ov011_02204428` (6) moving the `FX_Atan2` line, the `VEC_Add` before it and the `Ov011_MulVec33` call to every position changed nothing below 6. On `021fe2a4` (22), `const` on its three `.rodata` externs changed nothing. In `func_ov007_021ce038` (draft `scratchpad/big8/ce038/c.cpp`, 28 structural) the queue scans read `read`/`write` inside the inline (as in ov000's `CmdQueue_Has`), which took 92 to 84 aligned. The target there reloads the queue pointer for each of the four scans but keeps `read`/`write` in registers; the draft does the opposite. `FLD(u16, m, 0xc2) &= ~0x20` gives `and` with a pool constant where the target has `bic #0x20`: a 1-bit field at bit 5 gives the `bic` (`bf.cpp`, 27 structural), but it moves every register (481 aligned).

## TU2 string-pool blockers, round 2 (round mainpool2)

None of the nine TU2 blockers matched this round, so TU2 is still not merged. Drafts are in `scratchpad/mainpool2/d/`.

- **Pointer arithmetic on a constant pointer is only partly folded.** `func_02059c98` (box collision mesh for an `ObjPool` entry) computes its allocation size as `mov r1, #0x10; add r1, r1, #0x9c; add r5, r1, #0x390`. Integer arithmetic in any form (`+=`, separate statements, a loop, `register`, `sizeof`/`offsetof`, `u64`) folds to one constant. Pointer steps of different types do not fold across statements:

  ```c
  u8 *p = (u8 *)0x10;   /* header */
  BoxTri *t;
  p += 0x9c;
  t = (BoxTri *)p;
  t += 12;              /* 12 triangles of 0x4c bytes */
  size = (s32)t;
  ```

  This gives the target's three instructions. `(u8 *)0x10 + 0x9c` in one expression is folded by the front end, and two `u8 *` steps in a row are merged.
- **What `func_02059c98` is.** It calls `func_02059b14`, sets `kind = 4`, and allocates a `"TCOL"` mesh resource (`func_0203de84(0x45c, 4)`). The resource has `ofsHeader = 0x41c`, 12 triangles, a scale matrix (`MTX_Scale43_`/`MTX_Inverse43` at +0x48/+0x78), and `HitBounds` at +0x34. It then builds the 8 corners of the entry's box as `s16` vertices (`FX_Div(size, scale)`, where scale is `0x1000` or `FX_Div(max, 0x7fff) + 0x19a`). Two triangles per face come from `func_0203ff94`, with face values from `0x02082a10` and index tables from `0x02082a78` (4th argument nonzero) or `0x02082b08`. Finally it does `new HitMesh` (`func_0203dd9c(0x98)` + `func_0203f80c`), `func_0203f990`, `func_0203f8d8`, sets a translation-only matrix with `func_0203f9dc`, and returns the entry. The three table addresses are plain `.word`s in the target (no symbols or relocations in `config/`). Together with `data_02082a28` (`StageCmd.cpp`) they look like TU2's `.rodata` (`0x02082a10`..`0x02082b98`; the next TU loads `0x02082b98`). The TU2 merge will have to account for that range. The draft is `9c98_best.cpp` (122 sequence diffs; plain `for` loops, verts declared before the attribute table and matrix for the target's stack layout, `s` used for both the size and the scale as in the target). Still open:
  - the register roles of `res`, `res + size` and `flip`;
  - both face loops are not rotated in the target (`b` to `cmp i, #6`). No `for`/`while`/inline/count-parameter form reproduced this. The only non-rotated constant loop found in matched code is `func_ov006_021e3f10`, where `i` reuses a register that already holds 0;
  - the mesh flag writes are `bic #8; orr #8` and `bic #2; orr #0`. Plain, signed, `bool` and `BOOL` bit-fields, inline setters, manual mask expressions and `static const` values all fold to a single `orr`/`bic`. MWCC only keeps a `bic` plus `orr` pair when it writes 1 to bit 0 of a unit (`n->a = 1` gives `bic #1; orr #1`), or for a signed field in a 32-bit unit;
  - the identity-plus-translation matrix is written with `stm r4!` pairs through a pointer in the target. An inline that takes a matrix pointer did not reproduce it.
- **`func_02060a08` needs a late `& 0xff` on the low byte.** The target masks the fourth key byte twice: once when it is passed as `u8` and once again right before the final `orr`. `*p = (d & 0xff) | ((a << 24) | ((u32)(b << 24) >> 8) | ((u32)(c << 24) >> 16));` inside `Flags_AddSlot(StageFlags *, u8, u8, u8, u8)` gives the target's instruction count (62 to 50 aligned diffs, `0a08_f1.cpp`). Writing `(u32)(d << 24) >> 24` gives `lsl`/`lsr` instead, and `u32` parameters drop the first mask. The remaining diffs are register choices plus one operand order: the target keeps `a << 24` in a register and folds `>> 8` into the `orr`, while ours folds `a << 24`. Bit-field keys (`u32 d:8, c:8, b:8, a:8`) give `bic` sequences, so the key is not a bit-field.
- **`func_0205e29c` (9).** The pool order (`data_ov000_021ccd64` before `data_02149efc`) and the store of `unk_14` follow from a single register swap at the top: the target puts `args[2]` in `r2` and the zero for `unk_80` in `r3`. `automover.sh` on `e29c.cpp` found nothing that helps.
- **`func_0205b68c` (StageRes get/set, 46 to 41).** `rso.py` reordered the declarations in the bit-get loop. Only register allocation in the two bit loops is left (`d/b68c.cpp.rsobest`).
- **`func_0205b1b4` (2).** The target holds the function pointer in `r2` while the zero is in `r1`. Moving the handler assignment after the stores gives `r2`, but it also moves the pool entry and the store (6 diffs). Inline setters, a local function-pointer variable, defining `func_0205bef8` or `data_0208bde8` in the file, and `MI_CpuClear8` changed nothing. The permuter (about 1100 iterations on a minimal C file) found only non-equivalent code.
## Inline return forms in a message dispatcher, and register rotations that would not move (ov000 and ov007, round big9)
